On the weak Borel chromatic number and cardinal invariants of the continuum Thanks: † The first author was supported by the Excellence Fellowship Program for International Postdoctoral Researchers of The Israel Academy of Sciences and Humanities, and by the National Research, Development and Innovation Office – NKFIH, grants no. 124749, 129211. ∗ The second author was supported by the Israel Science Foundation grant 1838/19. Abstract
. We prove that consistently, cov( OPEN ℳ ) < λ 𝟎 < λ 𝟏 < λ ∞ < 2 ℵ 0 \mathcal{M})<\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}<\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}<\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}}<2^{\aleph_{0}} , where λ 𝟎 \lambda_{\mathbf{0}} denotes the weak Borel chromatic number of the Kechris-Solecki-Todorčević graph 𝔾 0 \mathbb{G}_{0} , that is, the minimal cardinality of a 𝔾 0 \mathbb{G}_{0} -independent Borel covering of 2 ω 2^{\omega} , while λ 𝟏 \lambda_{\mathbf{1}} and λ ∞ \lambda_{\infty} are the corresponding invariants of the graph 𝔾 1 \mathbb{G}_{1} and the simple graph associated with the equivalence relation E 0 E_{0} .



0.
Introduction

Borel graphs and their combinatorial properties have become a growing area of research in the last two decades and it has interesting connections with other areas such as the theory of graph limits, countable group actions, paradoxical
decompositions, as well as ergodic theory.

The Borel chromatic number was studied and defined in
[
KST99
]
[
LM08
]
.
For a graph
G=(X,E)
on a Polish space
X
its Borel chromatic number
\chi_{\text{B}}(G)
is the least cardinal
\kappa
, such that for some Polish space
Y
there exists a Borel coloring
c:X\to Y
of
G
with
|{\rm ran}(c)|=\kappa
(i.e. for each
y\in Y
the preimage
c^{-1}(y)
is
G
-independent). It is clear by the theory of Polish spaces and the
Perfect Set Property
of analytic sets that this number is an element of the set
\{0,1,2,\dots\}\cup\{\aleph_{0},2^{\aleph_{0}}\}
.

The theory was extended by S. Geschke, who showed that if
X
is Polish, then for each closed graph
G=(X,E)
without perfect cliques, as well as for each locally countable
F_{\sigma}
graph
G=(X,E)
(i.e. each node has degree at most
\aleph_{0}
) there exists some ccc. forcing making the continuum large, while
X
can be covered by
\aleph_{1}
-many Borel (in fact, closed)
G
-independent sets
[
Ges11
]
. Later M. Gaspar- S. Geschke
[
GG22
]
have defined the weak Borel chromatic number of a fixed graph
G
as the least possible cardinal
\kappa
, such that the underlying space can be covered by
\kappa
-many
G
-independent Borel sets. Note that if either chromatic number is at most countable, then they coincide.
(Here we remark that S. Geschke had defined the weak Borel chromatic number (of the graph
G=(X,E)
) as the smallest cardinal
\kappa
, such that there exists a coloring
c:X\to\kappa
with Borel fibers (i.e. for each
\alpha<\kappa
the preimage
c^{-1}(\alpha)
is Borel)
[
Ges11
]
. Note that if for a fixed graph either variant of the weak Borel chromatic number is at most
\aleph_{1}
, then they coincide.)

In the celebrated paper of A. Kechris-S. Solecki- S. Todorčević
[
KST99
]
the graph
\mathbb{G}_{0}
(with
X=2^{\omega}
) was constructed, and proved to be minimal among analytic graphs of uncountable Borel chromatic number in the sense that for each simple graph
G=(Y,F)
, where
Y
is Polish,
E\in\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1}_{1}(Y^{2})
either
\chi_{\text{B}}(G)\leq\aleph_{0}
, or there exists a continuous homomorphism
f:2^{\omega}\to Y
from
\mathbb{G}_{0}
to
G
(i.e. whenever
(x,x^{\prime})\in E_{\mathbb{G}_{0}}
, then
(f(x),f(x^{\prime}))\in F
holds necessarily). This also implies that whenever
G=(Y,F)
is an analytic graph on a Polish space with uncountable weak Borel chromatic number, it is at least as the weak Borel chromatic number of
\mathbb{G}_{0}
.

While the graph
\mathbb{G}_{0}
is acyclic, so it can be colored by two colors, B. Miller showed that the measurable chromatic number of it is
3

[
Mil08
]
. He asked whether anything can be said about the weak Borel chromatic number of
\mathbb{G}_{0}
compared to other cardinal characteristics of the continuum.
In
[
KST99
]
not only has the authors verified that
\chi_{\text{B}}(G)>\aleph_{0}
, but it also followed from their argument, that each
\mathbb{G}_{0}
-independent Baire-measurable set
S\subseteq 2^{\omega}
must be meager. This immediately implies that cov(
\mathcal{M}
) is a lower bound for
the weak Borel chromatic number of
\mathbb{G}_{0}
as well.

Due to M. Gaspar and S. Geschke, independently of this work, various Borel chromatic numbers of graphs were computed in models of set theory obtained by forcing with countable support iteration of uniform tree-forcing notions
[
GG22
]
, or see further results by R. Banerjee, M. Gaspar
[
BG22
]
. Earlier F. Adams and J. Zapletal had studied cardinal invariants of closed graphs
[
AZ18
]
.

1.
Preliminaries, notations

Under ordinals we always mean von Neumann ordinals, and for a set
X
the symbol
|X|
always refers to the smallest ordinal with the same cardinality. For a set
X
the symbol
{\mathscr{P}}(X)
denotes the power set of
X
, while if
\kappa
is an cardinal we use the standard notation
[X]^{\kappa}
for
\{Y\in{\mathscr{P}}(X):\ |Y|=\kappa\}
, similarly for
[X]^{<\kappa}
,
[X]^{\leq\kappa}
, etc. By a sequence we mean a function on an ordinal, where for a sequence
\overline{s}=\langle s_{\alpha}:\ \alpha<{\rm dom}(\overline{s})\rangle
the length of
\overline{s}
(in symbols
{\ell g}(\overline{s})
) denotes
{\rm dom}(\overline{s})
. We denote the empty sequence by
\langle\rangle
. Moreover, for sequences
\overline{s}
,
\overline{t}
, we let
\overline{s}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\overline{t}
denote the natural concatenation of them (of length
{\ell g}(\overline{s})+{\ell g}(\overline{t})
).
For a set
X
, and ordinal
\alpha
we use
{}^{\alpha}X=\{\overline{s}:\ {\ell g}(\overline{s})=\alpha,\ {\rm ran}(\overline{s})\subseteq X\}
, and for cardinals
\lambda
,
\kappa
we use the symbol
\lambda^{\kappa}=|^{\kappa}\lambda|
(that is, the least ordinal equivalent to it).

For a finite sequence
\bar{s}\in\ ^{\omega>}2
the symbol
[\bar{s}]
stands for the basic open set in
2^{\omega}
that
\bar{s}
determines, i.e.

[\bar{s}]=\{x\in\ ^{\omega}2:\ x\supseteq\bar{s}\}.

A tree
T
is a downward closed set consisting of finite sequences.

If
\varphi(n,x)
is a formula, then
\forall^{\infty}n\varphi(n,x)
is true, if for all but finitely many
n\in\omega

\varphi(n,x)
is true,
\exists^{\infty}n
stands for there exists infinitely many
n
, and we use the quantifier
\exists!y
as “there exists a unique
y
”.
For
r,r^{\prime}\in\omega^{\omega}
under
r\leq^{*}r^{\prime}
we mean that
\forall^{\infty}n

r_{n}\leq r^{\prime}_{n}
.

Concerning forcing,
q\leq p
means that
q
is stronger, and for a notion of forcing
{\mathbb{P}}
the term
1_{\mathbb{P}}
stands for the unique largest element of
{\mathbb{P}}
.

2.
The forcing construction

Let
\bar{\mathfrak{s}}=\langle\mathfrak{s}_{n}:\ n\in\omega\rangle
be fixed, such that

(\text{x}_{1})

for each
n\in\omega
:
\mathfrak{s}_{n}\in\ ^{n}\omega
,

(\text{x}_{2})

the set
\bigcup_{n\in\omega}[\mathfrak{s}_{n}]
is dense in
2^{\omega}
.

Recall the definition of the graph
\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{s})
on the Cantor space
[
KST99
]
:

Definition 2.1
.

The graph
\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})
is defined as follows:

\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})=\{(\mathfrak{s}_{n}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 0\rangle\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}x,\mathfrak{s}_{n}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 1\rangle\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}x):\ n\in\omega,\ x\in 2^{\omega}\}\subseteq[2^{\omega}]^{2}.

Theorem 2.2
.

(
[
KST99
]
)
For any sequence
\bar{t}
satisfying

(

x
1

)

,

(

x
2

)

the graph
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}(\bar{t})\subseteq[2^{\omega}]^{2}
is a closed acyclic graph such that whenever
H\subseteq 2^{\omega}
has the Baire property and
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}(\bar{t})
-independent, then it must be meager.

Moreover, if
G=(X,E)
is an analytic graph on the Polish space
X
and
\chi_{\text{B}}(G)>\aleph_{0}
, then there exists a continuous map
f:2^{\omega}\to X
, which is a homomorphism from
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}(\bar{t})
into
G
.

From now on we will only write
\mathbb{G}_{0}
instead of
\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})
. Note that the graph
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}(\bar{s})
enjoys the expected properties if

(

x
1

)

,

(

x
2

)

holds without any regard to the specific sequence
\bar{\mathfrak{s}}
, justifying the use of the terminology
\chi_{\text{B}}({\mathbb{G}}_{0})
,
\chi_{\text{wB}}({\mathbb{G}}_{0})
,
{\rm cov}(I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{0}})
.

Definition 2.3
.

The graph
\mathbb{G}_{1}
is defined as follows:

\mathbb{G}_{1}=\{(x,y):\ x,y\in 2^{\omega},\ \exists!n\in\omega\ x_{n}\neq y_{n}\}\subseteq[2^{\omega}]^{2}.

Definition 2.4
.

The Vitali relation
E_{0}
is defined as follows:

E_{0}=\{(x,y):\ x\neq y\in 2^{\omega},\ \forall^{\infty}n\in\omega\ x_{n}=y_{n}\}\subseteq[2^{\omega}]^{2}.

Note that this is not the standard definition of the Vitali relation, as we interpret it as a subset of
[2^{\omega}]^{2}
, while in the literature
E_{0}\subseteq 2^{\omega}\times 2^{\omega}
is an equivalence relation.

Definition 2.5
.

If
X
is a topological space, and
G
is a graph on it, then we let
I_{G}\subseteq{\mathscr{P}}(X)
denote the
\sigma
-ideal generated by Borel
G
-independent sets.

Now we are ready to state our main theorem.

Theorem 2.6
.

Assume
\mathbf{CH}
, and let
\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}\leq\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}\leq\lambda_{\infty}\leq\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}=\kappa
be infinite cardinals such that
\lambda_{\iota}=\lambda_{\iota}^{\aleph_{0}}
for each
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}
.
Then in some cardinal preserving forcing extension we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}{\rm cov}(\mathcal{M})=\mathfrak{d}&=\aleph_{1},\\
{\rm cov}(I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{0}})&=\lambda_{\mathbf{0}},\\
{\rm cov}(I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}})&=\lambda_{\mathbf{1}},\\
{\rm cov}(I_{E_{0}})&=\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}},\\
\ 2^{\aleph_{0}}&=\kappa=\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}.\end{array}

Proof.

We define our forcing posets in the following steps.

Definition 2.7
.

(\text{D}{1})

For each
n
we let
C_{n}\subseteq 2^{<\omega}
be a finite, non-empty set such that for each
n

(a)

x\neq y\in C_{n}

\to

x\nsubseteq y
or
y\nsubseteq x
,

(b)

C_{2n+1}=\{\langle 0\rangle,\langle 1\rangle\}
, and

(c)

for each
\bar{t}\in\prod_{i<2n}C_{i}
there exists
\bar{t}^{\prime}\in C_{2n}
such that
\bar{t}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{s}_{k}
(from

(

x
1

)

-

(

x
2

)

) for some
k\in\omega
.

(This can be achieved by induction, e.g. before constructing
C_{2n}
imposing the auxiliary demand that for some
r\in 2^{\omega}

\bar{t}\in C_{2n}\ \to\ r\notin[\bar{t}]
.)

(\text{D}{2})

Let
p\in\mathbb{P}^{\mathbf{0}}
, if

(
i
)

p=\langle p_{i}:\ i\in\omega\rangle
, where
\forall i:\ \emptyset\neq p_{i}\subseteq C_{i}
, and

(
ii
)

\exists^{\infty}j
:
p_{2j}=C_{2j}

\wedge

p_{2j+1}=C_{2j+1}
,

with
q
stronger than
p
, (in symbols,
q\leq p
) iff
q_{i}\subseteq p_{i}
for each
i
.

(\text{D}{3})

For
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}
just recall the definition of the Silver real forcing: we let
p\in\mathbb{P}^{\mathbf{1}}
, if

(
i
)

p=\langle p_{i}:\ i\in\omega\rangle
, where
\forall i:\ \emptyset\neq p_{i}\subseteq\{0,1\}
, and

(
ii
)

\exists^{\infty}j
:
p_{j}=\{0,1\}
,

with
q
stronger than
p
, (in symbols,
q\leq p
) iff
q_{i}\subseteq p_{i}
for each
i
.

(\text{D}{4})

We let
p\in\mathbb{P}^{\mathbf{\infty}}
, if

(
i
)

p=\langle p_{i}:\ i\in\omega\rangle
, where
\forall i\in\omega:\ \emptyset\neq p_{i}\subseteq\ ^{k_{p_{i}}}2
for some
k_{p_{i}}>0
, and

(
ii
)

\exists^{\infty}j
:
|p_{j}|=2
,

with
q
stronger than
p
, (in symbols,
q\leq p
) iff

•

there exists a strictly increasing infinite sequence
j_{0}<j_{1}<\dots
of finite ordinals, for which

(\forall i<\omega):\ k_{q_{i}}=k_{p_{j_{i-1}+1}}+k_{p_{j_{i-1}+2}}+\dots+k_{p_{j_{i}}},

where under
j_{-1}
we mean
-1
,

•

for each
i<\omega
,
\bar{t}\in q_{i}
there exists

(\bar{t}^{*}_{j_{i-1}+1}\in p_{j_{i-1}+1})\ \&\ (\bar{t}^{*}_{j_{i-1}+2}\in p_{j_{i-1}+2})\ \&\dots\&\ (\bar{t}^{*}_{j_{i}}\in p_{j_{i}}),

such that

\bar{t}=\bar{t}^{*}_{j_{i-1}+1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}t^{*}_{j_{i-1}+2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}t^{*}_{j_{i}}.

(So the biggest element
p
can be described as the condition satisfying for each
j

p_{j}=\{\langle 0\rangle,\langle 1\rangle\}
.)

(\text{D}{5})

If
p\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\iota}
(

),

for some

, then let

denote the condition defined as

for

.

(\text{D}{6})

Moreover, let
{\mathbb{P}}^{-m}
denote the subforcing
\{p{\restriction}[m,\omega):\ p\in{\mathbb{P}}\}
of
{\mathbb{P}}
with the natural order.

Recall the definition of the Sacks forcing, and so let
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbb{S}}=\{T\subseteq 2^{<\omega}:\ T\text{ is a perfect tree}\}
with
T\leq T^{\prime}
iff
T\subseteq T^{\prime}
.

Definition 2.8
.

(\text{D}{7})

For
n\in\omega

on
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}

and

(\text{D}{8})

For

on

as

, iff

•

q\leq p
, and

and

\forall j<n:\ p_{l_{j}}=q_{l_{j}}=\{0,1\},

(\text{D}{9})

For

and

(\forall k<l_{n-1})\ p_{k}=q_{k},

(\text{D}{10})

For

(so

to be the minimal branching node of

(i.e.

and stem(

,

, but each proper initial segment of

(\text{D}{11})

We define the partial order
\leq_{n}
(for every

) on
{\mathbb{S}}

as

•

,

•

,
{\rm stem}(q)={\rm stem}(p)
, and for this common stem

q^{[s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 0\rangle]}\leq_{n}p^{[s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 0\rangle]},\text{ and}

q^{[s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 1\rangle]}\leq_{n}p^{[s\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 1\rangle]}.

A standard argument yields the following.

Observation 2.9
.

{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}
,
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}
,
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}
,
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbb{S}}
satisfy Baumgartner’s Axiom A with the partial orders defined above in

⁢
D
7

)

and

⁢
D
11

)

, in particular if we are given the sequence

p_{0}\geq_{0}p_{1}\geq_{1}p_{2}\geq_{2}\dots\geq_{n-1}p_{n}\geq_{n}p_{n+1}\geq_{n+1}\dots,

then there exists a common lower bound
p^{\prime}
w.r.t.
\geq
(in fact even

can be assumed for each

).

(\text{D}{12})

For
I\subseteq\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
we let

{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{0}}_{I}=\{f\in\ ^{I}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}):\ f(i)=1_{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}}\text{ for all, but countable }i^{\prime}s\}

be the countable support product of
{\mathbb{P}}
’s.

(\text{D}{13})

For
I\subseteq[\lambda_{\mathbf{0}},\lambda_{\mathbf{1}})

{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{1}}_{I}=\{f\in\ ^{I}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}):\ f(i)=1_{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}}\text{ for all, but countable }i^{\prime}s\}

be the countable support product of
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}
’s.

(\text{D}{14})

Similarly for
I\subseteq[\lambda_{\mathbf{1}},\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}})
let

{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}_{I}=\{f\in\ ^{I}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}):\ f(i)=1_{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}}\text{ for all, but countable }i^{\prime}s\}

be the countable support product of
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}
’s,

(\text{D}{15})

and for
I\subseteq[\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}},\lambda_{\mathbf{{\mathbb{S}}}})
let

{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{{\mathbb{S}}}}_{I}=\{f\in\ ^{I}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{{\mathbb{S}}}}):\ f(i)=1_{{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{{\mathbb{S}}}}}\text{ for all, but countable }i^{\prime}s\}

be the countable support product of
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{{\mathbb{S}}}}
’s.

(\text{D}{16})

We let
{\mathbb{Q}}
be the following countable support product:

{\mathbb{Q}}={\mathbb{Q}}^{0}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{1}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}^{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}\setminus\lambda_{\infty}}.

We have to check that the forcing
{\mathbb{Q}}
is indeed cardinal preserving, forcing
\mathfrak{d}
to be
\aleph_{1}
, the continuum to be
\kappa=\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}
, there exists a system of
\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
-many
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}
-independent Borel sets covering
2^{\omega}
, but any system of smaller cardinality is not sufficient (and similarly for
{\mathbb{G}}_{1}
, and
E_{0}
).
For this we will prove the following:

(\circledast)_{1}

{\mathbb{Q}}
is proper, and has the
\aleph_{2}
-cc,

(\circledast)_{2}

{\mathbb{Q}}
is
\omega^{\omega}
-bounding, i.e. for each
r\in\ ^{\omega}\omega\cap V^{\mathbb{Q}}
there exists
r^{\prime}\in\ ^{\omega}\omega\cap V
such that
r^{\prime}\geq^{*}r
,

(\circledast)_{3}

for each
r\in\ ^{\omega}2\cap V^{\mathbb{Q}}
there exists a

•

tree
T_{\mathbf{0}}\in V^{{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{0}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}}\cap\mathscr{P}(2^{<\omega})
such that
r\in[T_{\mathbf{0}}]
, and
[T_{\mathbf{0}}]
is
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}
-independent,

•

tree
T_{\mathbf{1}}\in V^{{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{0}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{1}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}}\cap\mathscr{P}(2^{<\omega})
such that
r\in[T_{\mathbf{1}}]
, and
[T_{\mathbf{1}}]
is
{\mathbb{G}}_{1}
-independent,

•

tree
T_{\mathbf{\infty}}\in V^{{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{0}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{1}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}}\times{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}_{\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}}}\cap\mathscr{P}(2^{<\omega})
such that
r\in[T_{\mathbf{\infty}}]
, and
[T_{\mathbf{\infty}}]
is
E_{0}
-independent,

(\circledast)_{4}

If
\alpha<\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}
,
T\in V^{{\mathbb{Q}}{\restriction}\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}\setminus\{\alpha\}}\cap{\mathscr{P}}(^{\omega>}\{0,1\})
is a tree, such that

•

either
\alpha<\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
, and
[T]
is
{\mathbb{G}}_{0}
-independent,

•

or
\alpha\in[\lambda_{\mathbf{0}},\lambda_{\mathbf{1}})
, and
[T]
is
{\mathbb{G}}_{1}
-independent,

•

\alpha\in[\lambda_{\mathbf{1}},\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}})
, and
[T]
is
E_{0}
-independent,

then for
r_{\alpha}
, the generic real given by the
\alpha
’th coordinate (
{\mathbb{Q}}{\restriction}\{\alpha\}
), we have:

r_{\alpha}\notin[T].

Similar statements to

(
⊛
)

3

are proved independently in
[
GG22
, §3]
(for an extension adding a single real, and generalizing it to CS iterations), and see also
[
Zap19
, Thm 3.47- Corollary 3.49]
, a more general result, albeit only for a single step extension, which is independent of both.

Observe that the properness of
{\mathbb{Q}}
(together with our assumptions on the ground model) would imply that
(2^{\aleph_{0}})^{V^{{\mathbb{Q}}{\restriction}\lambda_{\mathbf{\iota}}}}=\lambda_{\iota}
(
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}
), and so
(2^{\aleph_{0}})^{V^{\mathbb{Q}}}=\kappa^{\aleph_{0}}=\kappa
(
=\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}
), while it follows from

(
⊛
)

3

that cov(
I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{0}})\leq\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
(and the respective inequalities similarly hold for
{\mathbb{G}}_{1}
, and
E_{0}
).

By the
\aleph_{2}
-cc of
{\mathbb{Q}}
, if
\mu
is uncountable, then each system of Borel sets of size
\mu
is in

V^{{\mathbb{Q}}{\restriction}M}
for some
M\in V
of size at most
\mu\cdot\aleph_{1}=\mu
.

Moreover,

(
⊛
)

2

clearly implies
\mathfrak{d}^{V^{\mathbb{Q}}}=\aleph_{1}
, and the inequality
{\rm cov}(\mathcal{M})\leq\mathfrak{d}
holds in
\mathbf{ZFC}
(since each compact set in
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathbb{N}}
is meager as well, or see
[
BJ95
]
).
Finally, since each uncountable Borel subset of a Polish space is a continuous image of (a closed subspace of)
{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathbb{N}}

[
Kec12
, Theorems 7.9, 13.1]
, each Borel set is the union of
\mathfrak{d}
-many compact sets. Thus, if a Polish space can be covered by
\aleph_{1}\leq\mu
-many Borel sets, then we can replace each Borel set
B
with a system of
\aleph_{1}
-many compact sets
\langle K_{\alpha}:\ \alpha<\omega_{1}\rangle
with
B=\bigcup_{\alpha<\omega_{1}}K_{\alpha}
, so (in the extension) there exists
\langle K^{\prime}_{\alpha}:\ \alpha<
cov(
I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{0}}
)
\rangle
with each
K^{\prime}_{\alpha}
compact, covering
2^{\omega}
. This together with

(
⊛
)

4

implies cov(
I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{0}}
)
\geq\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
, cov(
I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{1}}
)
\geq\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}
, cov(
I_{E_{0}}
)
\geq\lambda_{\mathbf{\infty}}
.
Therefore it is indeed enough to verify clauses

(
⊛
)

1

-

(
⊛
)

4

.

Claim 2.10
.

{\mathbb{Q}}
has the
\aleph_{2}
-cc.

Proof.

Suppose that
\langle a_{i}:\ i<\omega_{2}\rangle
is an antichain. Since
|{\rm supp}(a_{i})|\leq\aleph_{0}
for
i<\omega_{2}
, by
\mathbf{CH}
we can assume that
\{{\rm supp}(a_{i}):\ i<\omega_{2}\}
forms a
\Delta
-system with kernel
K
. But
|{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}|=|{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}|=|{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}|=|{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbb{S}}|=|(2^{\aleph_{0}})^{V}|=\aleph_{1}
(by Definition
2.7
), so
|^{K}({\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}\cup{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{1}}\cup{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{\infty}}\cup{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbb{S}})|=\aleph_{1}
, we are done.
∎

Convention 2.11
.

By passing down to a dense subset of
{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}
, from now on we can assume that whenever
p\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}
,
k\in\omega
,

\neg(p_{2k}=C_{2k}\ \wedge\ p_{2k+1}=C_{2k+1})\ \rightarrow\ |p_{2k}|=|p_{2k+1}|=1.

Definition 2.12
.

(\text{D}^{enumi}{1})

if
p\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}
,
n\in\omega
, and
d
is the smallest integer for which

for some
l_{0}<l_{1}<\dots<l_{n-1}<d
we have

\forall j<n:\ (p_{2l_{j}}=q_{2l_{j}}=C_{2l_{j}})\&(p_{2l_{j}+1}=q_{2l_{j}+1}=C_{2l_{j}+1}),

then we let

T_{n}(p)=\prod_{j<2d}p_{j},

and

T(p)=\bigcup_{n\in\omega}T_{n}(p),

(\text{D}^{enumi}{2})

if
p\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}
,
\bar{u}\in\bigcup_{n\in\omega}\prod_{j<n}p_{j}
, (e.g.

), then we define

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}

,

.

(\text{D}^{enumi}{3})

for
n\in\omega
,

is the minimal natural number such that for some

then for each

(\text{D}^{enumi}{4})

(\text{D}^{enumi}{5})

for

,

we define the node

by induction on

\bar{\mathfrak{t}}_{\langle\rangle}(p)=\langle\rangle,

and

\begin{array}[]{rl}\bar{\mathfrak{t}}_{\bar{s}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 0\rangle}(p)=&{\rm stem}\left(p^{[\bar{\mathfrak{t}}_{\bar{s}}(p)]}\right)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 0\rangle,\\

Note that

Observation 2.13
.

If
p\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}
,
i\in\omega
,

(2.3)

p_{0}=\{\bar{t}^{p}_{0}\},p_{1}=\{\bar{t}^{p}_{1}\},p_{i-1}=\{\bar{t}^{p}_{i-1}\},

and we consider the condition
p^{\prime}\leq p
defined as

\begin{array}[]{l}p^{\prime}_{0}=\{\bar{t}^{p^{\prime}}_{0}=\bar{t}^{p}_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}^{p}_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}^{p}_{i-1}\},\\
p^{\prime}_{j+1}=p_{j+i}\ (\text{for }j\in\omega)\end{array}

then
p\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}^{\infty}}p^{\prime}\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
p^{*}\leq p
, and
p^{*}\perp p^{\prime}
. By further strengthening
p^{*}
w.l.o.g. we can assume that
|p^{*}_{0}|=1
, and for the unique
\bar{t}^{*}\in p^{*}_{0}
we have

{\ell g}(\bar{t}^{*})>|\bar{t}^{p}_{0}|+|\bar{t}^{p}_{1}|+\dots+|\bar{t}^{p}_{i-1}|.

But then this bound together with

p^{*}\leq p
and (
2.3
) implies that

\bar{t}^{*}=(\bar{t}^{p}_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}^{p}_{1}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}^{p}_{i-1})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}t^{p}_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots t^{p}_{i+k}=\bar{t}^{p^{\prime}}_{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}^{p}_{i}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots\bar{t}^{p}_{i+k}

for some
\bar{t}^{p}_{i}\in p_{i}=p^{\prime}_{1}
,
\bar{t}^{p}_{i+1}\in p_{i+1}=p^{\prime}_{2}
,
\dots
,
\bar{t}^{p}_{i+k}\in p_{i+k}=p^{\prime}_{k+1}
, so not only is
p^{\prime}
compatible with
p^{*}
, but
p^{*}\leq p^{\prime}
.

∎

Definition 2.16
.

Observe that

(2.4)

\text{whenever }\bar{\bar{\ell}}\in\mathcal{L}:\ |\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(q)|<\aleph_{0},

Definition 2.17
.

Assuming the sequences
\bar{\varepsilon}^{\iota}
are as in Definition
2.15
(
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}

\bar{\bar{\ell}}\in\mathcal{L}

\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}
defined by

\begin{array}[]{rl}p\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}q\ \iff&\bullet_{1}\ (p\leq q)\ \wedge\\
&\bullet_{2}\ \mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(p)=\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(q)\ \wedge\\
&\bullet_{3}\ \forall(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})\in\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(q):\ p^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}\leq q^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}.\end{array}

Note the following easy corollaries of our definitions:

Observation 2.19
.

If
p,q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\bar{\bar{\ell}}\in\mathcal{L}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}^{\iota}
(
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}
) are as in Definition
2.15
,
(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})\in\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(p)
, and
q\leq p^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}
then for some
q_{*}\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}p
,

q_{*}^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}\Vdash q\in\mathbf{G},

even
q_{*}^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}=q
, if in addition
p^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}({\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{j})\geq_{\ell^{\infty}_{j}}q({\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{j})
holds for each
j
.

Moreover, we can assume that whenever
m\in\omega
, and
s^{\prime}\in\ ^{\omega>}2
is not comparable with
s_{m}
(i.e.
s^{\prime}\nsubseteq s_{m}
,
s^{\prime}\nsupseteq s_{m}
), then
q_{*}({\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{0}}_{m})\in{\mathbb{P}}^{\mathbf{0}}
satisfies
q_{*}({\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{0}}_{m})^{(s^{\prime})}=p({\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{0}}_{m})^{(s^{\prime})}
.

Note that we cannot expect above
q_{*}^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}=q
to hold in general, since on coordinates of the form
{\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{j}
possibly
q({\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{j})_{0}=\{\bar{t}\}
, where
\bar{t}=(\bar{t}^{*})^{0}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}(\bar{t}^{*})^{1}
with
(\bar{t}^{*})^{0}\in q_{*}^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}({\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{j})_{0}
, and
(\bar{t}^{*})^{1}\in q_{*}^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}({\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{j})_{1}
.

Observation 2.20
.

If
p\geq q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\bar{\bar{\ell}}\in\mathcal{L}
,
(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})\in\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(q)
, then
q^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}\leq p^{(\bar{u}^{\prime},\bar{v}^{\prime},\bar{w}^{\prime},\bar{s}^{\prime})}
for some
(\bar{u}^{\prime},\bar{v}^{\prime},\bar{w}^{\prime},\bar{s}^{\prime})\in\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}(p)
.

The next claim verifies the properness part of

(
⊛
)

1

, and

(
⊛
)

2

.

Claim 2.21
.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
,
D_{0},D_{1},\dots,D_{i},\dots
be a countable sequence of maximal antichains of
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Then for a suitable extension
q^{\prime}\leq q
we have that for each
i\in\omega

q^{\prime}
is compatible with only finitely many elements of
D_{i}
.

Proof.

Assume that
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
, and the
D_{j}
’s are fixed. In what follows we will
sketch a standard fusion argument for Baumgartner’s Axiom A.

The following is a trivial application of Observation
2.9
:

Observation 2.22
.

Suppose that the sequence
\langle q_{n}:\ n\in\omega\rangle\in\ ^{\omega}{\mathbb{Q}}
is decreasing, the sequences
\bar{\varepsilon}^{\iota}
(
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}
) are as in Definition
2.15
, and for each
k
there is
\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{k}=((\bar{\ell}^{k})^{\mathbf{0}},(\bar{\ell}^{k})^{\mathbf{1}},(\bar{\ell}^{k})^{\mathbf{\infty}},(\bar{\ell}^{k})^{\mathbb{S}})\in\mathcal{L}
such that

•

for each
\alpha\in\bigcup_{n\in\omega}{\rm supp}(q_{n})
we have that
\alpha={\varepsilon}^{\iota}_{j}
for some
\iota
and
j
,
and

\langle(\ell^{k})^{\iota}_{j}:\ k\in\omega\rangle\text{ is nondecreasing, converging to }\infty,

•

q_{n+1}\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q_{n}
holds for each
n
.

Then there exists a common lower bound
q_{\omega}\in{\mathbb{Q}}
of the sequence
\langle q_{n}:\ n\in\omega\rangle
.
Moreover, there exists
q_{\omega}
for which for each
n

q_{\omega}\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q_{n}
.

We will define the sequences
\langle q_{i}:\ i<\omega\rangle
,
\langle{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{0}}_{i}:\ i\in\omega\rangle
,
\langle{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{1}}_{i}:\ i\in\omega\rangle
,
\langle{\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{i}:\ i<\omega\rangle

\langle{\varepsilon}^{\mathbb{S}}_{i}:\ i<\omega\rangle
,
\langle\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{i}:\ i\in\omega\rangle
satisfying the following:

(\XBox_{1})

q_{0}=q
, and for each
i
we have
q_{i}\in{\mathbb{Q}}
,

(\XBox_{2})

\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{0}}_{i}:\ i\in\omega\}\subseteq\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
,
\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{1}}_{i}:\ i\in\omega\}\subseteq\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
,
\{{\varepsilon}^{\infty}_{i}:\ i<\omega\}\subseteq\lambda_{\infty}\setminus\lambda_{\mathbf{1}}
,
\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbb{S}}_{i}:\ i<\omega\}\subseteq\lambda_{\mathbb{S}}\setminus\lambda_{\infty}
,

(\XBox_{3})

for each
n

{\rm supp}(q_{n})\subseteq\{{\varepsilon}^{\iota}_{j}:\ \iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\},\ j\in\omega\}
,

(\XBox_{4})

for each
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}
and
j\in\omega
the sequence

\langle(\ell^{n})^{\iota}_{j}:\ n\in\omega\rangle\ \text{ is nondecreasing, and tends to }\infty,

(\XBox_{5})

\forall n

q_{n}\geq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q_{n+1}
,

(\XBox_{6})

\forall n
the condition
q_{n+1}
is compatible with only finitely many conditions in
D_{n}
.

Provided that such sequences exist we can appeal to Observation
2.22
, which will complete the proof of Claim
2.21
.

We can clearly define a sequence of
\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{i}
’s as in Observation
2.22
.
Now by Observation
2.18
, and some standard bookkeeping arguments it is easy to see that the entire induction can be done once we specify how to define the condition
q_{n+1}\in{\mathbb{Q}}
from
q_{n}
and the adequate fragment of
\bar{\varepsilon}^{\iota}
’s.
This
q_{n+1}
will satisfy that

(
\blacktriangle_{1}
)

q_{n+1}\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q_{n}
,

(
\blacktriangle_{2}
)

whenever
(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})\in\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}(q_{n})
,
then
(q_{n+1})^{(\bar{u},\bar{v},\bar{w},\bar{s})}
is compatible with exactly one element of
D_{n}
.

For this

(\circledcirc_{1})

let

M=|\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}(q_{n})|,

and
fix an enumeration

(2.5)

\langle(\bar{u}^{i},\bar{v}^{i},\bar{w}^{i},\bar{s}^{i}):\ i<M\rangle

of
\mathbf{seq}_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}(q_{n})
.

Note that
(

▲
2

)
includes
M
-many different objectives, each one is corresponding to some
(\bar{u}^{i},\bar{v}^{i},\bar{w}^{i},\bar{s}^{i})
from
(
2.5
)
.
So

(\circledcirc_{2})

we construct the sequence
\langle q^{*}_{i}:\ i\leq M\rangle

satisfying

q^{*}_{0}=q_{n}\geq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q^{*}_{1}\geq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}\dots\geq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q^{*}_{M},

and

(\forall i<M):\ (q^{*}_{i+1})^{(\bar{u}^{i},\bar{v}^{i},\bar{w}^{i},\bar{s}^{i})}\Vdash p_{*}\in\mathbf{G},\ \text{ for some }p_{*}\in D_{n},

thus
q^{*}_{M}
will work (i.e.
(

▲
1

)
,
(

▲
2

)
hold).

Assuming that
i<M
and
q^{*}_{i}
is defined,
pick
q^{\prime}\leq(q^{*}_{i})^{(\bar{u}^{i},\bar{v}^{i},\bar{w}^{i},\bar{s}^{i})}
, such that
q^{\prime}\leq p_{*}
for some
p_{*}\in D_{n}
. Let

q^{*}_{i+1}\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}^{n}}q^{*}_{i}
,
(q^{*}_{i+1})^{(\bar{u}^{i},\bar{v}^{i},\bar{w}^{i},\bar{s}^{i})}\Vdash p_{*}\in\mathbf{G}
(guaranteed by Observation
2.19
). Clause

(

⊚
2

)

clearly holds, so we are done.

∎

We can turn to the proof of

(
⊛
)

3

:

Claim 2.23
.

For the forcing
{\mathbb{Q}}
defined above clause

(
⊛
)

3

holds.

Proof.

Fix a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-name

\textstyle z

\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}

By Claim
2.21
(and a standard density argument) we can assume, that

(\XBox_{1})

\textstyle z

\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}

is a
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}=\prod_{\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\infty,{\mathbb{S}}\}}{\mathbb{Q}}^{\iota}_{X_{\iota}}

moreover, w.l.o.g.

(\XBox_{2})

\Vdash_{{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\notin V
.

(\XBox_{3})

Fix enumerations
X_{\mathbf{0}}=\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{0}}_{j}:\ j\in\omega\}
,
X_{\mathbf{1}}=\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{1}}_{j}:\ j\in\omega\}
,
X_{\mathbf{\infty}}=\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbf{\infty}}_{j}:\ j\in\omega\}
,
X_{\mathbb{S}}=\{{\varepsilon}^{\mathbb{S}}_{j}:\ j\in\omega\}

(\XBox_{4})

If
{\varepsilon}\in X_{\mathbf{1}}\cup X_{\infty}\cup X_{\mathbb{S}}
,
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}
,
\bar{s}\in\ ^{\omega>}2
we let

be defined as

\begin{array}[]{rl}q^{\{{\varepsilon}\},(\bar{s})}\upharpoonright X_{\mathbf{0}}\cup X_{\mathbf{1}}\cup X_{\infty}\cup X_{\mathbb{S}}\setminus\{{\varepsilon}\}=&(q\upharpoonright X_{\mathbf{0}}\cup X_{\mathbf{1}}\cup X_{\infty}\cup X_{\mathbb{S}}\setminus\{{\varepsilon}\}),\\
q^{\{{\varepsilon}\},(\bar{s})}({\varepsilon})=&(q({\varepsilon})^{(\bar{s})}).\end{array}

(\XBox_{5})

If
{\varepsilon}\in X_{\mathbf{1}}\cup X_{\infty}\cup X_{\mathbb{S}}

q\leq_{\{{\varepsilon}\},n}p

We will again need the terminology introduced in Definition
2.15
.

Definition 2.24
.

If
\Vdash_{{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\in 2^{\omega}

Definition 2.25
.

Definition 2.26

Note the following:

Fact 2.27
.

If
\Vdash_{{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle z$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\in 2^{\omega}
,
n\in\omega
,
\iota\in\{\mathbf{1},\infty\}

Proof.

Observe that

(\blacksquare_{1})

if
p\geq r\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}
, then
\odot_{1}^{m}(p)\to\odot^{m}_{1}(r)
, and similarly,
\odot^{m}_{2}(p)\to\odot^{m}_{2}(r)
for every
m\in\omega
.

Note that

(\blacksquare_{2})

if for
p\in{\mathbb{Q}}
there is no extension
p^{\prime}\leq p
with
\odot^{m}_{1}(p^{\prime})
, then

\odot^{m}_{2}(p)
holds (and conversely),

therefore,

(\blacksquare_{3})

for
n\in\omega

the set

D^{m}_{\odot}=\{p\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}:\ \odot^{m}_{1}(p)\ \vee\ \odot^{m}_{2}(p)\}

is dense open (and the sets
\{p\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}:\ \odot^{m}_{1}(p)\}
,
\{p\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\prime}:\ \odot^{m}_{2}(p)\}
are open).

Fact 2.30
.

For every

Proof.

Using

(

■
3

)

choose

∎

Since
\leq_{\bar{\bar{\ell}}}
is a partial order, enumerating the finite set

). Thus

works.
∎

.

.

p^{\prime}\Vdash\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\in[\bar{t}_{**}],

so we can assume that
[t_{**}]\cap[T_{\iota}]\neq\emptyset
.
Consider

•

the sets

T_{\bar{t}_{**}(0)}=\{\bar{t}\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2:\ \bar{t}_{**}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 0\rangle\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}\in T\},

and

T_{\bar{t}_{**}(1)}=\{\bar{t}\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2:\ \bar{t}_{**}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 1\rangle\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}\in T\},

if
\iota=\mathbf{0}
or
\mathbf{1}
,

•

while if
\iota=\infty
, then let

T_{\bar{t}_{**}(0)}=\{\bar{t}\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2:\ \bar{t}_{**}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{j}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}\in T\},

and

T_{\bar{t}_{**}(1)}=\{t\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2:\ \bar{t}_{**}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{j}^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}\in T\},

(where
p^{\prime}_{j}=\{\bar{t}_{j}^{\prime},\bar{t}_{j}^{\prime\prime}\}
recalling (
2.48
)).

Now as
[T_{\mathbf{0}}]
(
[T_{\mathbf{1}}]
,
[T_{\mathbf{\infty}}]
, resp.) is

{\mathbb{G}}_{\mathbf{0}}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})
-independent (
{\mathbb{G}}_{\mathbf{1}}
-,
E_{0}
-independent, resp.) compact set for which
t_{**}\in T
, there must be
k\in\omega
such that
the sets
T_{\bar{t}_{**}(0)}\cap\ ^{k}2
, and
T_{\bar{t}_{**}(1)}\cap\ ^{k}2
are disjoint.

Now by further extending
p^{\prime}
if necessary we can assume that

•

(if
\iota=\mathbf{0}
)

|p^{\prime}_{2j+2}|=|p^{\prime}_{2j+3}|=\dots=|p^{\prime}_{2j+k+1}|=1
, and if
p^{\prime}_{2j+2+i}=\{\bar{t}_{2j+2+i}\}
(
i<k
), then the sequence

\bar{t}_{***}=\bar{t}_{2j+2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{2j+3}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{2j+k+1}\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2

is obviously of length
\geq k
.

•

(if
\iota=\mathbf{1}
)

|p^{\prime}_{j+2}|=|p^{\prime}_{j+3}|=\dots=|p^{\prime}_{j+k+1}|=1
, and if
p^{\prime}_{j+2+i}=\{a_{j+2+i}\}
(
i<k
), then the sequence

\bar{t}_{***}=\langle a_{j+2+i}:\ i<k\rangle\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2

is of length
k
,

•

(if
\iota=\mathbf{\infty}
)

|p^{\prime}_{j+2}|=|p^{\prime}_{j+3}|=\dots=|p^{\prime}_{j+k+1}|=1
, and if
p^{\prime}_{2j+2+i}=\{\bar{\bar{t}}_{2j+2+i}\}
(
i<k
), then the sequence

\bar{t}_{***}=\bar{t}_{2j+2}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{2j+3}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\dots\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{2j+k+1}\in\ ^{\omega\geq}2

is obviously of length
\geq k
.

If
\iota\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
, then
let
a\in\{0,1\}
be such that
t_{***}{\restriction}k\in T_{\bar{t}_{**}(a)}
.
Our observation above means that
t_{***}{\restriction}k\notin T_{\bar{t}_{**}(1-a)}
, thus

(2.49)

\bar{t}_{**}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 1-a\rangle\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{***}\notin T_{\iota}.

Extend
p^{\prime}
to
p^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{Q}}
such that
p^{\prime\prime}_{2j+1}=\{\langle 1-a\rangle\}
(if
\iota=\mathbf{0}
), or .
p^{\prime\prime}_{j+1}=\{1-a\}
(if
\iota=\mathbf{1}
),
and then

p^{\prime\prime}\Vdash\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle r$\crcr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\in[\bar{t}_{**}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\langle 1-a\rangle\mathbin{\mathchoice{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}{\text{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\smallfrown$}}}}\bar{t}_{***}]

which together with
(
2.49
)
contradicts
(
2.47
)
.
We can also reach the same contradiction in the case
\iota=\infty
, just working with
p_{j}=\{\bar{t}_{j}^{\prime},\bar{t}_{j}^{\prime\prime}\}
instead of
\{0,1\}
.

∎

Problem 2.45
.

Is it true, that in the final model there is a partition of the Cantor space into
\lambda_{\mathbf{0}}
-many
\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})
-independent Borel sets (while the other assertions from Theorem
2.6
still hold)? Is it consistent that there is a partition of
2^{\omega}
into
\lambda
-many
\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})
-independent Borel sets, where
{\rm cov}({\mathcal{M}})<\lambda<2^{\aleph_{0}}
, and less than
\lambda
-many (or just not more than
{\rm cov}({\mathcal{M}})
-many)
\mathbb{G}_{0}(\bar{\mathfrak{s}})
-independent Borel sets do not cover
2^{\omega}
?
What can we say about the corresponding invariant of
\mathbb{G}_{1}
, or
E_{0}
?

Problem 2.46
.

Define the graph

{\mathbb{G}}_{n}=\{(x,y)\in\ [^{\omega}2]^{2}:\ |\{j\in\omega:\ x_{j}\neq y_{j}\}|\leq n\}

for
n\in\omega
fixed.
Can we separate
{\rm cov}(I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{n}})
and
{\rm cov}(I_{{\mathbb{G}}_{n+1}})
?
Can we separate infinitely many
{\rm cov}(T_{{\mathbb{G}}_{j}})
’s?

3.
